/goal Generalize the channel-endpoint reference-counting facility into a user-facing OPT-IN deterministic reference-counted object kind (`@RefCounted`, Swift-ARC style), so any annotated type gets DETERMINISTIC `Drop` at strong-count 0 — promoting the deterministic-finalization carve-out that channel `Sender`/`Receiver` handles already use (see ROADMAP "deterministic endpoint release" + docs/16 §8) from a hardcoded runtime special-case into a real language feature. Implement at full Rust/Swift quality, no shortcuts: a refcounted-object header/descriptor variant carrying the strong count; ARC-style retain/release insertion across codegen (bind/copy/param/return/capture + heap stores via the docs/16 §7 write barriers); count→0 runs the type's `Drop` synchronously then frees; and the tracing GC retained as the CYCLE-collector backstop (refcounting leaks cycles — the collector reclaims them, Python-style). Ask first before finalizing the surface syntax / any language-design decision (this is a genuine spec addition — add a new docs section and reconcile with docs/16 §8). [DONE] /goal Implement `await`/suspension in genuinely-conditional positions: the right operand of `&&`/`||` and the condition of a `while` loop (today these correctly error "await in this position is not yet supported" rather than miscompiling). Follow docs/21 (async) exactly and DO NOT change the language design without asking first. The hard part is preserving evaluation order and conditional suspension frequency — a suspending op in `a && b` must only run when `a` is true, and a `while` condition suspends once per iteration. Build the lowering at full production quality (likely extending sema/anf.rs and the async state machine), no shortcuts. Remember async is implicit (the `spawn` keyword), so this is about the suspend points the fixpoint pass discovers, not surface syntax. [DONE] /goal Allow async closures in `Thread.spawn` (docs/20 §1, §4; docs/21 §6) — APPROVED design change: `Thread.spawn` accepts either a synchronous `() => R` or an async `() => Future<R>` closure, and always returns `JoinHandle<R>` (joining on the AWAITED `R`). Implemented: new `lang_thread_spawn_async(env, pending_tid)` runtime entry (closure-call + block_on-drive); checker detects the async closure (return type `Future<T'>`) and yields `JoinHandle<T'>`; backend `emit_thread_spawn` calls the new entry (pending_tid, no float_kind) when async; the Shared-lock rejection is narrowed to SYNCHRONOUS workers (async workers may await + lock); trailing `Thread.spawn { async => … }` parses; `JoinHandle.detach()` implemented (`lang_thread_detach`); a captured channel endpoint is owned by the future (released on completion, not when the building closure returns) so a worker can await-then-send/recv across a suspension. Tests: runtime drive (immediately-ready + suspend/resume), compiler (async→JoinHandle<T'>, async worker CAN lock, sync worker still errors, trailing form), parser (trailing async forms), LSP (hover), e2e concurrency (lock, parallel, detach, cross-thread channel, GC-stress) + native parity. Known limitation: one OS thread per async worker; worker-panic isolation remains separate.

[DONE] /goal Implement worker-panic isolation for threads and `spawn` workers (docs/20, docs/14): a panicking worker must fail only itself and surface as `Panicked{message}` to the joiner, NOT abort the whole process. — DONE: host unwinding can't cross Cranelift frames (no unwind tables), so each worker runs its body under a `setjmp`/`longjmp` PANIC BOUNDARY at its entry (`crates/runtime/src/panic_boundary.c`, built by `build.rs` via the `cc` crate; Rust glue in `panic_boundary.rs`). `lang_panic` checks `otter_pb_active()`: on a worker it captures the message and `longjmp`s back to the boundary (restoring the saved context directly across the generated frames — no frame-walk); on the MAIN thread no boundary is installed, so a top-level panic stays fatal (exit 101). The boundary (`run_under_boundary`) restores the invariants the longjmp skipped: drains held `Shared` locks (`lang_shared_release_all`, no poisoning) + the thread's transient cross-`poll` GC pins (new `gc::pin_for_unwind`/`unpin_for_unwind`/`release_unwind_pins`, used by `block_on`), then materialises the message as a pinned `str`. `finish_worker` publishes `Panicked{message}` so `JoinHandle.join()` surfaces it recoverably, while a `spawn EXPR` awaiter has the panic RE-PROPAGATED at its `await` (`spawn_poll`) per docs/21 §11. Covers `Thread.spawn` (sync + async closures) and the `spawn` keyword. JIT + native parity (C shim bundled into both the rlib and libruntime.a). No language-design decision needed (behavior was already specified in docs/14 §7, docs/16 panic row, docs/20 §4, docs/21 §11). Tests: 5 runtime unit/integration (panic_boundary + real worker-panic with sibling survival) + 4 CLI integration (isolated join + message, lock-release-no-poison, `spawn` propagation exits non-zero, GC-stress parity) + e2e `concurrency/{worker_panic_sibling_survives,worker_panic_gc_stress,spawn_panic_propagates,lock_released_on_panic}` (last graduated from XFAIL); full workspace + 260-case e2e suite green, 0 XFAIL. [DONE] /goal Resolve `@Variadic` extern functions (docs/19 §13), which was blocked because Cranelift lacks portable varargs-ABI support (aarch64 Apple passes varargs on the stack; Cranelift uses registers). — DONE via `libffi` (option A, user-approved). Confirmed Cranelift 0.132 has zero variadic support (`ir::Signature` = `{params, returns, call_conv}`, no fixed/variadic boundary, no `%al` mechanism), so a variadic call genuinely cannot be lowered as an ordinary call on the targets we emit (aarch64-apple-darwin stack passing, x86-64 SysV `%al`, Windows x64 float duplication). Implementation: the backend (`gen_hir::h_variadic_call`) marshals each argument — C-default-promoted in variadic position (`f32`→`f64`, sub-int/`bool`/`char`→`int`, `@Transparent` seen through to its inner scalar via `transparent_inner`), sign/zero-extended into a flat 8-byte-slot value buffer with a parallel type-tag array — gets the callee address via `func_addr`, and calls the runtime shim `lang_variadic_call` (`crates/runtime/src/variadic.rs`), a minimal hand binding to the *system* `libffi` (neither `pkg-config` nor autotools is available here, but `-lffi` links cleanly; `ffi_cif` is treated as an opaque over-sized buffer, the only target constant `FFI_DEFAULT_ABI` verified against ffitarget.h) that drives `ffi_prep_cif_var`/`ffi_call`. JIT links libffi via the runtime build script (`cargo:rustc-link-lib=ffi`); native `otter_fusion build` links `-lffi` when the program declares any `@Variadic`. Checker (`sema/check`): `validate_variadic` rejects `@Variadic` off an extern import / on an export-with-body / with no fixed prefix / with decorator args; `check_variadic_call` relaxes call arity to `>= n_fixed` and rejects non-passable variadic args (aggregates, `str` — pass a `*u8`). Used as a value (not called by name) the import is an ordinary non-variadic fn pointer over its fixed prefix (safe, no soundness hole). The LSP inherits the fix (shared `analyze_multi_ctx` checker). Verified against real `printf`/`snprintf`: int/str/double/char/hex, i64/u64 full width, f32→double promotion, negative sign-extension, `@Transparent` newtype, no-vararg path — JIT + native parity + GC-stress all identical. Tests: 2 runtime unit tests (drive `lang_variadic_call` against `snprintf` directly), 9 checker unit tests, 4 e2e `run` cases (`tests/cases/ffi/variadic_*`), 7 e2e compile-error cases (`tests/cases/errors/variadic_*`), 1 native-parity test (`run.rs`). Full workspace + 272-case e2e suite green. Docs (docs/19 §7 `@Variadic` subsection) and ROADMAP updated.

[DONE] /goal Add OS-backed `std:rand` RNG handles. — DONE: `std:rand` is now a mixed module: `SeededRng` remains the deterministic Otter-authored PRNG, while `OsRng`, `ThreadRng`, `RandomError`, `os_rng()`, `thread_rng()`, and `random_error()` are exported through the stdlib catalog. The runtime now exposes a target entropy hook (`lang_rand_os_u32`) backed by `getrandom`; `OsRng` provides fallible `try_next_u32`, `try_next_u64`, and `try_fill_bytes` methods returning `RandomError`, plus a non-fallible `Rng` impl that panics only if the provider entropy source fails. `ThreadRng` is a per-value generator seeded from `OsRng` and implements `Rng`; `RandomError` implements `std:error.Error`, equality, clone, hash, stringification, and debug rendering. Expanded `std_rand_basic.otter` to exercise deterministic RNGs, OS-backed handles, thread RNG construction, generic helper calls, and error value semantics; expanded near-empty-prelude coverage for `OsRng`/`thread_rng`. Updated docs/17, docs/18, docs/24, docs/29, ROADMAP, and goals so OS entropy/per-value RNG handles are implemented while cryptographic-strength API guarantees and richer distributions remain planned. [DONE] /goal Add runtime-backed `core:sync/atomic.AtomicPtr<T>`. — DONE: added `AtomicPtr<T>` to `core:sync/atomic` as an owned `@RefCounted` native atomic raw-pointer cell with deterministic `Drop`, shared handle cloning/capture semantics, target-backed typed `load`, `store`, `swap`, and `compare_exchange` operations, and the same operation/order validation used by the integer and bool atomic wrappers. The public API preserves the pointee type as `*T`, while the runtime stores an opaque `AtomicPtr<u8>` machine pointer; pointer arithmetic is intentionally not part of the surface. Registered JIT symbols, added runtime unit coverage, stdlib e2e coverage for typed pointer load/swap/store/CAS and import gating, cross-thread `Thread.spawn` pointer-store coverage using a scalar address to preserve raw-pointer capture safety, invalid store-order panic coverage, native parity for the threaded pointer case, LSP member-completion coverage, and docs/ROADMAP updates. This completes the currently documented atomic handle family. Verification: `cargo test -p runtime atomic::tests -- --nocapture`, `cargo test -p compiler sema::stdlib::tests -- --nocapture`, `cargo test -p lsp server::tests::member_completion_lists_core_atomic_ptr_methods -- --nocapture`, `cargo run -p cli -- run tests/cases/stdlib/core_sync_atomic_ptr_basic.otter`, `cargo run -p cli -- run tests/cases/stdlib/core_sync_atomic_ptr_threaded.otter`, direct panic run for `core_sync_atomic_ptr_invalid_ordering_panics.otter` produced `panic: invalid atomic store ordering`, native build/run parity via `cargo run -p cli -- build tests/cases/stdlib/core_sync_atomic_ptr_threaded.otter -o /tmp/otter_fusion_atomic_ptr_threaded_native && /tmp/otter_fusion_atomic_ptr_threaded_native`, full CLI e2e suite green (`465 cases · 465 pass · 0 fail · 0 XFAIL`), and `cargo fmt`.

[DONE] /goal Add live `std:process.Child` handles. — DONE: added runtime-backed `Command.spawn(): Child | IoError` to `std:process`, plus a deterministic `@RefCounted` `Child` handle with shared clone semantics, provider process `id()`, cached `wait(): ExitStatus | IoError`, and `kill(): null | IoError`. The runtime stores live children in a handle table, releases the table entry when the last Otter handle drops, avoids holding the child-table mutex while blocking in OS `wait`, and preserves Rust/process semantics by not implicitly killing the OS process on handle drop. Since `Command` has no pipe configuration surface yet, `Child.stdin()`, `stdout()`, and `stderr()` return `null`; streamed child stdio and richer target status details remain planned. Registered JIT symbols, added runtime tests for spawn/wait/repeated wait/release and kill/wait, added e2e coverage for successful spawn, cached wait, cloned-handle wait, missing/invalid command errors, and kill, expanded `std:process` near-empty-prelude coverage for `Child`, added LSP member-completion coverage, and updated docs/17, docs/18, docs/24, docs/29, ROADMAP, and goals. Also scoped the parser so the documented method name `spawn` is accepted in interface/extend method declarations while top-level `function spawn()` remains rejected because `spawn` is an expression keyword. Verification: `cargo test -p runtime process::tests -- --nocapture`, `cargo test -p compiler --test parser spawn_keyword -- --nocapture`, `cargo test -p compiler sema::stdlib::tests -- --nocapture`, `cargo test -p lsp server::tests::member_completion_lists_std_process_child_methods -- --nocapture`, direct JIT run for `std_process_child_basic.otter`, direct compile-error check for `std_process_requires_import.otter`, native build/run parity via `cargo run -p cli -- build tests/cases/stdlib/std_process_child_basic.otter -o /tmp/otter_fusion_process_child_basic && /tmp/otter_fusion_process_child_basic`, and full CLI e2e suite green (`466 cases · 466 pass · 0 fail · 0 XFAIL`).

[DONE] /goal Add synchronous provider-backed TCP streams/listeners to `std:net`. — DONE: added mixed `TcpStream` and `TcpListener` handles backed by provider TCP sockets. Both are deterministic `@RefCounted` runtime handles with shared clone semantics and explicit `close()`; `TcpStream` implements synchronous `Reader`/`Writer` plus `connect`, `peer_addr`, `local_addr`, and `set_nodelay`, while `TcpListener` implements `bind`, `accept(): (TcpStream, SocketAddr) | IoError`, and `local_addr`. [DONE] /goal Add formatter wrapping for long shift chains. — DONE: `compiler::fmt` now wraps long top-level shift value-expression chains at binary `<<`/`>>` token boundaries, reusing the shared infix-chain renderer at the parser's shift precedence tier between multiplicative/additive operands and bitwise-AND chains. The wrapper is deliberately conservative: comment/string/interpolation guardrails still apply, it only splits lines with at least two top-level shift operators whose neighboring tokens look like operands, it ignores generic-angle `>>` closes, and it refuses lines with top-level lower-precedence bitwise/logical/comparison operators so mixed-precedence expressions are not made visually misleading. general:

/goal Analyze the current state of the project, go thought each doc, read all the necessary source code, run tests if needed. Then, update roadmap, ensure roadmap is up to date. Be detailed. We will use the roadmap to complete goals in goals.txt.

/goal Finish Otter Fusion end to end as a production-ready language, using ROADMAP.md and the docs as the authoritative backlog. Complete every remaining core language, compiler, runtime, backend, standard-library, package-manager, tooling, LSP/editor, documentation, example, and test-suite item recorded in the roadmap or design docs, one test-gated slice at a time. This includes closing the advanced deferrals that still block a truly complete toolchain: deeper backend/MIR-or-equivalent optimization work, any remaining std/core provider and standard-library surfaces, package-manager deferred features, LSP follow-ups, formatter/comment-preservation work, GC throughput work that remains behavior-neutral, and roadmap Phase 7 embedding-engine work unless explicitly moved out of scope by a design decision. Follow AGENTS.md and the docs; do not take shortcuts or silently change language design. Preserve clean layering, modular architecture, semantics, GC/rooting/finalizer safety, async/task/channel correctness, deterministic `@RefCounted` behavior, observability (`otter_fusion emit tokens|ast|hir|clif`, DWARF, `run --time`/`exec --time`), JIT/native parity, examples, docs, and the VS Code/LSP experience. Every completed slice must include focused unit/integration/e2e tests, negative/regression coverage where relevant, benchmark or timing coverage for performance-sensitive work, docs/examples/ROADMAP updates, and goals.txt bookkeeping. This umbrella goal is not done until ROADMAP.md has no remaining unimplemented core feature, advanced deferral, documented surface, or required quality gate except items explicitly and intentionally deferred beyond the production-ready language scope.

optimizations:

/goal Push Otter Fusion from the current production-safe backend optimization pass toward a deeper optimizing compiler. Design and implement a MIR/SSA-oriented optimization pipeline or equivalent backend optimization layer, staged and test-gated, with interprocedural escape analysis, broader inlining with real cost modeling and recursion/code-growth guards, constant folding and propagation, dead-store/dead-branch elimination, loop-aware optimizations where safe, richer devirtualization/monomorphization of statically-known dynamic/interface paths, target-aware lowering improvements, and tighter stdlib/package code emission where it fits the existing module/provider architecture. Preserve language semantics, GC/rooting/finalizer safety, `@RefCounted` deterministic release behavior, async/task/channel correctness, and all observability (`otter_fusion emit tokens|ast|hir|clif`, DWARF, `run --time`/`exec --time`). Prove JIT/native parity and optimization soundness with focused backend unit tests, CLI integration tests, e2e cases, negative/regression tests for safety boundaries, GC-stress/concurrency cases where relevant, and benchmark coverage for every optimization slice. Keep docs, ROADMAP, examples, and goals consistent.

new features:

/goal Design and build the full standard library as production infrastructure, not a bag of compiler magic. Stage it module-by-module according to docs/18 and docs/24: keep `core:*` compiler/runtime substrate and language-privileged utilities separate from `std:*` official library APIs and target-backed implementations; allow modules to be authored in Otter Fusion, Rust-backed runtime/compiler code, or mixed layers; route `core:*`/`std:*` availability through the stdlib catalog/provider architecture so future target/custom sysroots can replace, omit, or extend `std:*` packages without changing source-level import syntax; progressively move toolchain library definitions out of the monolithic embedded `PRELUDE_SRC` into maintainable std/core source modules or an equivalent well-structured toolchain package; keep the near-empty prelude rule; implement each module with unit, integration, and e2e tests, docs, examples, and LSP support. Current priority slices per ROADMAP.md and docs/18/docs/24: named-zone timezone database/conversion extensions and leap-second policy for `std:time`; async network adapters and richer socket options for `std:net`; streamed child stdio and richer process status details for `std:process`; cryptographic-strength APIs and distribution helpers for `std:rand`; remaining collection/bytes/deque surfaces; provider/catalog/sysroot hardening; and any later documented std/core surfaces that are not already implemented. Do not change language design without asking.



/goal Implement the embedding engine `std:engine` (docs/26, ROADMAP Phase 7): run guest Otter Fusion inside a sandboxed isolate. Follow docs/26 exactly and the locked design decisions — bridged data crosses by COPY-BY-VALUE over a `@Bridge` repr-C layout (never a shared heap pointer), and resource isolation uses PER-ISOLATE runtime state (each isolate owns its heap/GC/registries, selected by a thread-local current-isolate pointer). Build on the proven macro-host substrate (crates/macros + crates/backend/macro_host). Production quality, no shortcuts; ask first before any language-design change. Stage it per ROADMAP Phase 7 (each step shippable): (1) single-isolate load/invoke returning primitives; (2) compile-time capability-whitelist enforcement of std:/core:/pkg:/file:; (3) the `@Bridge` ABI (frozen repr-C layout, bridge-compatibility checking, field-by-field copy marshalling, str-as-bytes); (4) host bindings into virtual `host:` modules + engine-managed bridge channels/Shared spanning the boundary + Unit entry introspection; (5) per-isolate runtime state threaded through every lang_* entry point with per-isolate stop-the-world GC; (6) hard limits (max_heap_bytes/max_alloc_bytes/timeout_ms/max_stack_depth via per-isolate accounting + deadline/cancel flag at existing safepoints) + Stats; (7) async entries driven on the isolate executor from the host's await (no user-visible block_on). Do many tests — unit + integration + e2e: capability denial as a compile error, @Bridge round-trips for every bridge type (host↔guest), host bindings + bridge channels, arbitrary-entry invocation by name, per-isolate heap independence, hard memory cap → OutOfMemory, timeout → Timeout (incl. tight-loop latency), guest-panic containment → GuestPanic, async entry awaited by a host; JIT + native parity + GC-stress. Keep docs/26, examples, LSP and ROADMAP consistent and up to date.
